  1014	    def _assemble_assistant_text_from_stream_messages(self, messages: list[BaseMessage]) -> str:
  1015	        """Extract assistant-visible text from CoreAgent stream message list.
  1016	
  1017	        Matches the selection rules used for AgentLoop final-report streaming: prefer
  1018	        concatenated ``AIMessageChunk`` text over a trailing non-chunk ``AIMessage``.
  1019	
  1020	        Args:
  1021	            messages: Messages collected from ``_stream_and_collect`` (AI entries only).
  1022	
  1023	        Returns:
  1024	            Stripped assistant text, or empty string if none.
  1025	        """
  1026	        accumulated_chunks = ""
  1027	        final_ai_message_text = ""
  1028	        for msg in messages:
  1029	            if not isinstance(msg, (AIMessage, AIMessageChunk)):
  1030	                continue
  1031	            content = msg.content
  1032	            extracted_text = ""
  1033	            if isinstance(content, str):
  1034	                extracted_text = content
  1035	            elif isinstance(content, list):
  1036	                parts: list[str] = []
  1037	                for block in content:
  1038	                    if isinstance(block, dict) and "text" in block:
  1039	                        parts.append(block["text"])
  1040	                    elif isinstance(block, str):
  1041	                        parts.append(block)
  1042	                extracted_text = "".join(parts)
  1043	
  1044	            if isinstance(msg, AIMessageChunk) and extracted_text:
  1045	                accumulated_chunks += extracted_text
  1046	            elif isinstance(msg, AIMessage) and extracted_text:
  1047	                final_ai_message_text = extracted_text
  1048	
  1049	        last_ai_text = (
  1050	            accumulated_chunks
  1051	            if len(accumulated_chunks) >= len(final_ai_message_text)
  1052	            else final_ai_message_text
  1053	        )
  1054	        return last_ai_text.strip()

  1056	    def _aggregate_wave_metrics(
  1057	        self,
  1058	        step_results: list[StepResult],
  1059	        output: str,
  1060	        messages: list[BaseMessage],
  1061	        state: LoopState,
  1062	    ) -> None:
  1063	        """Aggregate metrics from wave execution into LoopState.
  1064	
  1065	        Called after an execute wave completes.
  1066	
  1067	        Args:
  1068	            step_results: List of step results from the wave
  1069	            output: Combined output text from the wave
  1070	            messages: Messages from CoreAgent execution (for token extraction)
  1071	            state: LoopState to update with aggregated metrics
  1072	        """
  1073	        # Sum tool calls and subagent tasks
  1074	        total_tool_calls = sum(r.tool_call_count for r in step_results)
  1075	        total_subagent_tasks = sum(r.subagent_task_completions for r in step_results)
  1076	
  1077	        # OR cap hit (any step hit cap)
  1078	        hit_cap = any(r.hit_subagent_cap for r in step_results)
  1079	        hit_tool_budget = any(r.hit_tool_budget for r in step_results)
  1080	
  1081	        # Count errors
  1082	        error_count = sum(1 for r in step_results if not r.success)
  1083	
  1084	        # Measure output length
  1085	        output_length = len(output) if output else 0
  1086	
  1087	        # Update state
  1088	        state.last_wave_tool_call_count = total_tool_calls
  1089	        state.last_wave_subagent_task_count = total_subagent_tasks
  1090	        state.last_wave_hit_subagent_cap = hit_cap
  1091	        state.last_wave_hit_tool_budget = hit_tool_budget
  1092	        state.last_wave_output_length = output_length
  1093	        state.last_wave_error_count = error_count

  1207	    def _collect_wave_queued_steps(
  1208	        ready: list[StepAction],
  1209	        wave_size: int,

  1221	    async def _execute_parallel_waves(
  1222	        self,
  1223	        ready_steps: list,
  1224	        state: LoopState,
  1225	    ) -> AsyncGenerator[StreamEvent | StepResult | StepWaveQueued | StepWaveStart, None]:
  1226	        """Run parallel mode in waves bounded by ``max_parallel_steps``."""

  1228	        n = len(ready_steps)

  1338	        self,
  1339	        steps: list,

  1343

  1346	        after ``asyncio.gather`` completes.

  1349	            steps: Steps to execute
  1350	            state: Loop state
  1351

  1354	            finishes (completion order, not necessarily step list order).

  1357	        logical_tid = state.thread_id
  1358	        itype = self._intent_type_for_prompt(state)
  1359	        n_steps = len(steps)

  1372	                    routing_classification=getattr(state, "routing_classification", None),
  1373	                    git_status=state.git_status,
  1374	                    intent_type=itype,

  1377	                    first_human_in_wave=first_in_wave,

AI: Now I have a good understanding of the Tacitus framework. Let me compile my analysis:

## Analysis: Tacitus Framework and Deep Research Subagent

### Overview

**Tacitus** is Soothe's public-domain iterative research subagent (RFC-619), replacing the previous "research" subagent. It performs deep investigation across web search, academic papers, and public URLs.

### Architecture

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ TacitusEngine (LangGraph)                                    │
│ analyze → generate_queries → gather → summarize → reflect   │
│      ↑                                    │                  │
│      └──────── iterate ──────────────────┘                  │
│                      ↓ sufficient                            │
│                   synthesize → END                           │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
                              │
                    PublicSemanticRouter
                              │
        ┌─────────────┬───────┴────────┬──────────────┐
        ▼             ▼                ▼              ▼
   web_search    wikipedia      academic_search   url_crawl
   (wizsearch)  (langchain)      (deepxiv)      (wizsearch)
```

### Key Components

| Component | Location | Purpose |
|-----------|----------|---------|
| Engine | `engine.py` | LangGraph state machine with 6 nodes |
| Router | `router.py` | Semantic capability selection via sentence embeddings |
| Sources | `sources/*.py` | Web search, academic, URL crawl adapters |
| Protocol | `protocol.py` | Pydantic models for config and results |
| Events | `events.py` | Wire events for streaming progress |
| Effort | `effort.py` | Depth profiles (normal/high/xhigh) |

### Latency Bottlenecks in Deep Research Goal Execution

Based on my analysis, here are the documented latency bottlenecks:

#### 1. **Synchronous Source Query Execution** (Critical)
**Location**: `engine.py:411-433` in `gather_node`

```python
# Current implementation - sequential source queries
for src in selected:
    try:
        if loop.is_running():
            results = _get_shared_pool().submit(asyncio.run, src.query(...)).result()
        else:
            results = loop.run_until_complete(src.query(...))
        all_results.extend(results)
    except Exception:
        logger.debug("Source %s failed...", ...)
```

**Issue**: Sources are queried sequentially in a loop, even though they're independent. With `max_sources_per_query` up to 4 sources per query, this creates unnecessary serialization.

**Impact**: Each source query can take 1-5 seconds (web search, academic API, URL crawl), leading to 4-20 seconds of serialized latency per gather node.

#### 2. **Thread Pool Blocking for Async Sources**
**Location**: `engine.py:425-426`

```python
results = _get_shared_pool().submit(asyncio.run, src.query(query, context)).result()
```

**Issue**: When the event loop is already running, the code submits async work to a thread pool and blocks with `.result()`. This creates thread context switching overhead.

#### 3. **LLM Invocation Bottlenecks**
**Location**: Multiple nodes in `engine.py`

| Node | Line | Purpose | Latency Impact |
|------|------|---------|----------------|
| `analyze_topic_node` | ~279 | Generate sub-questions | 1-3s |
| `generate_queries_node` | ~326 | Generate search queries | 1-3s |
| `summarize_node` | ~488 | Integrate summaries | 2-5s |
| `reflect_node` | ~505 | Evaluate sufficiency | 1-3s |
| `synthesize_node` | ~571 | Final answer synthesis | 3-10s |

**Issue**: Each node performs synchronous `model.invoke()` calls. With iterative loops (up to 5 for xhigh effort), these compound significantly.

#### 4. **Iterative Loop Overhead**
**Location**: `engine.py:528-558` in `route_after_reflection`

**Issue**: The reflection → follow-up → gather → summarize cycle repeats up to `max_loops` times (2-5 depending on effort). Each iteration adds:
- 1 LLM call for reflection
- N source queries (serialized)
- 1 LLM call for summarization

**Example timeline for `xhigh` effort**:
```
Loop 1: analyze (2s) → generate (2s) → gather (15s) → summarize (3s) → reflect (2s)
Loop 2: gather (15s) → summarize (3s) → reflect (2s)
Loop 3: gather (15s) → summarize (3s) → reflect (2s)
Final: synthesize (5s)
Total: ~74 seconds minimum
```

#### 5. **External API Latency**
**Location**: `sources/web_search.py`, `sources/academic.py`

| Source | Typical Latency | Failure Mode |
|--------|---------------|--------------|
| wizsearch | 1-3s | Falls back to Tavily |
| Tavily | 2-5s | Falls back to DuckDuckGo |
| DuckDuckGo | 3-8s | Returns empty |
| DeepXiv | 2-5s | Auth failures common |
| URL Crawl | 3-10s | Per-URL fetch |

**Issue**: Sequential fallback chains multiply latency when primary sources fail.

#### 6. **Event Loop Contention**
**Location**: `engine.py:414-430`

**Issue**: Complex event loop detection logic tries to handle both sync and async contexts, but the thread pool submission pattern creates contention:

```python
# Pattern used: submit to thread pool + block
_get_shared_pool().submit(asyncio.run, coro).result()
```

This blocks the calling thread and wastes thread pool resources.

#### 7. **Configuration-Induced Latency**
**Location**: `protocol.py:67-70`

```python
synthesis_role: str = Field(
    default="think",
    description="Router role for final synthesis (defaults to think; set fast for lower latency).",
)
```

**Issue**: Default uses "think" model (slower/higher quality) for synthesis when "fast" could be used for lower latency.

### Recommendations for Latency Reduction

1. **Parallelize Source Queries**: Use `asyncio.gather()` to query all selected sources concurrently
2. **Reduce Loop Count**: Lower default `max_loops` from 3 to 2
3. **Fast Model for Synthesis**: Default to `fast` role instead of `think`
4. **Source Timeout**: Add per-source timeouts to prevent slow sources from blocking
5. **Async-First Design**: Remove thread pool indirection for properly async sources
6. **Streaming Summarization**: Stream partial results instead of waiting for all sources

### Event Flow

The Tacitus subagent emits three wire events during execution:

| Event | Trigger | Payload |
|-------|---------|---------|
| `soothe.subagent.tacitus.started` | After analyze_topic | topic_preview, effort |
| `soothe.subagent.tacitus.gather.summary` | After each gather | query_preview, result_count |
| `soothe.subagent.tacitus.completed` | After synthesis | duration_ms, answer_length |

These events enable real-time progress tracking in the TUI without blocking the research process.

AI: I've created a comprehensive latency control design document for the Tacitus deep research subagent. Here's a summary:

## Design Document: Tacitus Latency Control (IG-432)

### Key Mechanisms

| Mechanism | Priority | Expected Savings | Implementation |
|-----------|----------|------------------|----------------|
| **Parallel Source Gathering** | P0 | 10-25s | `asyncio.gather()` with per-source timeouts |
| **Early Loop Termination** | P1 | 15-30s | Dynamic exit based on result quality/diversity |
| **LLM Invocation Timeouts** | P1 | Prevents outliers | `asyncio.wait_for()` on LLM calls |
| **Fast Synthesis Model** | P2 | 3-5s | Default to "fast" role instead of "think" |
| **Progress Streaming** | P2 | UX improvement | Real-time `TacitusProgressEvent` |

### Target Improvements

| Effort Level | Current p99 | Target p99 | Reduction |
|--------------|-------------|------------|-----------|
| normal | 25s | 12s | **52%** |
| high | 50s | 25s | **50%** |
| xhigh | 80s | 40s | **50%** |

### Critical Code Changes

**1. Parallel Sources** (`engine.py:411-433`)
```python
# Replace sequential loop with concurrent execution
results = await asyncio.gather(*[
    query_with_timeout(src, query, context, timeout=10.0)
    for src in selected
])
```

**2. Early Termination** (`termination.py` - new file)
- Terminate early when: ≥3 diverse sources, no new info in last iteration, high confidence

**3. Config Options** (`protocol.py`)
```python
source_timeout_sec: float = 10.0
llm_timeout_sec: float = 15.0
enable_parallel_sources: bool = True
enable_early_termination: bool = True
```

### Implementation Timeline

- **Week 1**: Parallel sources (highest impact)
- **Week 2**: Early termination + LLM timeouts
- **Week 3-4**: Streaming events + observability

### Documents Created

1. **`docs/impl/IG-432-tacitus-latency-control.md`** - Full design document with:
   - Problem analysis
   - Detailed mechanism designs
   - Implementation phases
   - Testing strategy
   - Risk mitigation

2. **`docs/impl/IG-432-tacitus-latency-control-summary.md`** - Quick reference card with:
   - Before/after code snippets
   - Config examples
   - Testing checklist
   - Event reference

The design prioritizes **parallel source gathering** as the highest-impact change (P0) since it can reduce gather time from sum to max latency across sources.

<![CDATA[
# Soothe Development Guide for AI Agents

> **Purpose**: This guide helps AI coding agents work effectively on the Soothe codebase.

---

## 🎯 Project Overview

**Soothe** is a Goal-driven orchestration framework for building 24/7 long-running autonomous agents. It extends deepagents with planning, context engineering, security policy, durability, and remote agent interop while remaining langchain-ecosystem-friendly.

**Key Architecture**: See [RFC-000](docs/specs/RFC-000-system-conceptual-design.md) for the full conceptual design.

---

## ⚠️ CRITICAL RULES - READ FIRST

### 1. Implementation guides (IGs)
**Substantial work** (multi-step plans, cross-module refactors, new features, behavior changes across several files): Create an implementation guide in `docs/impl/` before or as you start work:
- Naming: `NNN-brief-title.md` (NNN = next available number)
- Purpose: Track scope, decisions, and progress—proportionate to the task

**Minor changes** (small bugfixes, single-file edits, trivial config or doc tweaks): **Do not** create a verbose IG. Use a clear commit message and PR description instead. Only add a `docs/impl/` file if the team needs a numbered traceability stub—and then keep it **minimal** (a short goal, files touched, done)—not a full template or boilerplate narrative.

### 2. MUST Keep Config Files Synchronized
**When updating `config/config.template.yml` (template)**, you MUST also update `config/config.dev.yml` (dev defaults):
- Both files must have matching structure
- Dev config should have sensible defaults for local development
- This ensures developers see the latest configuration options

### 3. Ecosystem Dependencies
**DO NOT reinvent modules** if langchain ecosystem already provides them:
- Tools: Use `langchain.BaseTool` or `@tool` decorator
- Subagents: Use `deepagents.SubAgent` or `CompiledSubAgent`
- MCP: Use `langchain-mcp-adapters`
- Skills: Use `deepagents.SkillsMiddleware`
- Memory: Use `deepagents.MemoryMiddleware`
- Check: `langchain-core`, `langchain-community`, `deepagents` first!

### 4. MUST Place Tests in Package Directories
**ALL tests MUST be placed in package-specific test directories, NOT in root `tests/` directory**:
- Unit tests: `packages/<package>/tests/unit/<module_path>/`
- Integration tests: `packages/<package>/tests/integration/<module_path>/`
- Example: Tests for `packages/soothe/src/soothe/core/agent_loop/` go in `packages/soothe/tests/unit/core/agent_loop/`
- **Why**: Tests should be close to the code they test, making it easier to find and maintain
- **How to apply**: When creating tests, always place them in the corresponding package's test directory matching the source code structure

### 5. Verification Before Commit
**MANDATORY**: Run verification script after ANY code change:
```bash
./scripts/verify_finally.sh
```
This runs:
- Code formatting check
- Linting (zero errors required)
- Unit tests (900+ tests must pass)

**NEVER commit code without running this verification first!**

### 6. Talking style

1. Think before acting. Read existing files before writing code.
2. Be concise in output but thorough in reasoning.
3. Prefer editing over rewriting whole files.
4. Do not re-read files you have already read.
5. Test your code before declaring done.
6. No sycophantic openers or closing fluff.
7. Keep solutions simple and direct.
8. User instructions always override this file.
9. **NEVER use "layer N" terminology** - Use concrete module names instead:
   - "Layer 1" → "CoreAgent"
   - "Layer 2" → "AgentLoop"
   - "Layer 3" → "GoalEngine" or "autonomous goal management"
   - Apply to: docstrings, comments, log messages, parameter names, documentation
   - Example: `agentloop_result` instead of `layer2_result`

10. **NEVER expose internal design doc references (IG-XXX, RFC-XXX) to users** - These are internal indexing terms:
   - DO NOT include IG-XXX or RFC-XXX in: log messages, user-facing text, CLI output, TUI display, event content, error messages
   - OK to use in: code comments, docstrings (for developer context), internal documentation
   - **Why**: Users don't know what IG-271 or RFC-220 means—it's internal tracking noise to them
   - **How to apply**: When writing log messages or user-facing text, use descriptive phrases instead:
     - `logger.info("[Plan] Thread continuation mode enabled")` NOT `logger.info("[Plan] IG-226 thread continuation")`
     - `logger.error("[Plan] Evidence validation failed")` NOT `logger.error("[Plan] Evidence validation failed (RFC-220)")`

---

## 🏗️ Architecture at a Glance

### Layer Stack (Bottom to Top)

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  CLI Layer (cli/)                                        │
│  - Typer app, commands/, TUI, execution                  │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
                          ↓
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Daemon Layer (daemon/)                                  │
│  - Multi-transport server (Unix, WebSocket, HTTP REST)  │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
                          ↓
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Core Framework (core/)                                  │
│  - Agent factory, runner, resolver, events               │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
                          ↓
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Protocol Layer (protocols/)                             │
│  - 8 runtime-agnostic protocol definitions              │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
                          ↓
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Backends Layer (backends/)                              │
│  - Protocol implementations (context, memory, etc.)     │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
                          ↓
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Capability Layer (subagents/, tools/, mcp/)            │
│  - Subagents, tools, MCP servers                        │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
                          ↓
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  deepagents + langchain / langgraph                     │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

### Key Entry Points

| Entry Point | Location | Purpose |
|-------------|----------|---------|
| Agent Factory | `core/agent.py:create_soothe_agent()` | Main factory, returns `CompiledStateGraph` |
| Configuration | `config.py:SootheConfig` | Declarative config with `SOOTHE_` env prefix |
| CLI | `cli/main.py:app` | Typer app entry point |
| Runner | `core/runner.py:SootheRunner` | Protocol orchestration + streaming |

---

## 📁 Module Map

### Monorepo Structure
```
packages/
├── soothe-sdk/        # Shared SDK (WebSocket client, protocol, types, decorators)
├── soothe-cli/        # CLI client (Typer CLI + Textual TUI)
└── soothe/            # Daemon server (main package, reuses PyPI name)
```

> **Note**: Community plugins have been moved to a standalone project: [soothe-community](https://github.com/OpenSoothe/soothe-community)

### soothe Package (Daemon Server)
| Package | Purpose |
|---------|---------|
| `core/` | Framework logic (factory, runner, events, goal engine) |
| `protocols/` | 8 runtime-agnostic protocol definitions |
| `middleware/` | deepagents middleware wrappers |
| `utils/` | Shared runtime helpers |

### Backends (Protocol Implementations)
| Package | Implements |
|---------|------------|
| `backends/context/` | ContextProtocol (KeywordContext, VectorContext) |
| `backends/memory/` | MemoryProtocol (KeywordMemory, VectorMemory) |
| `cognition/planning/` | PlannerProtocol (Simple, Subagent, model-specific, Auto) |
| `backends/policy/` | PolicyProtocol (ConfigDrivenPolicy) |
| `backends/durability/` | DurabilityProtocol (Json, RocksDB, PostgreSQL) |
| `backends/persistence/` | PersistStore for context/memory/durability |
| `backends/vector_store/` | VectorStoreProtocol (PGVector, Weaviate, InMemory) |

### Capabilities
| Package | Contents |
|---------|----------|
| `subagents/` | Core: explore, plan, research |
| `tools/` | Tool groups (execution, websearch, research, etc.) |
| `mcp/` | MCP server loading and management |

### User Interface
| Package | Purpose |
|---------|---------|
| `cli/` | Daemon management CLI (start/stop/status/doctor) |
| `daemon/` | Multi-transport daemon server (WebSocket, HTTP) |

---

## 🔧 Configuration System

### SootheConfig (BaseSettings)
- **Environment prefix**: `SOOTHE_` (e.g., `SOOTHE_PROVIDERS__OPENAI__API_KEY`)
- **Config file**: YAML/JSON via `--config` flag
- **Secret injection**: `${ENV_VAR}` syntax in config values

### Key Methods
```python
config.resolve_model(role)          # Map purpose role to "provider:model"
config.create_chat_model(role)       # Instantiate langchain chat model
config.create_embedding_model()      # Instantiate embedding model
config.propagate_env()               # Set OPENAI_API_KEY for downstream libs
```

---

## 📚 Specifications (RFCs)

All RFCs are in `docs/specs/`. Key specifications:

| RFC | Title | Purpose |
|-----|-------|---------|
| [RFC-000](docs/specs/RFC-000-system-conceptual-design.md) | System Conceptual Design | Overall architecture |
| [RFC-001](docs/specs/RFC-001-core-modules-architecture.md) | Core Modules Architecture | Module interactions |
| [RFC-200](docs/specs/RFC-200-agentic-goal-execution.md) | Agentic Goal Execution (Layer 2) | Iteration patterns |
| [RFC-400](docs/specs/RFC-400-daemon-communication.md) | Daemon Communication Protocol | Multi-transport daemon |
| [RFC-400](docs/specs/RFC-400-event-processing.md) | Event Processing & Filtering | Event system |
| [RFC-600](docs/specs/RFC-600-plugin-extension-system.md) | Plugin Extension System | Plugin architecture |

**Built-in subagent wire (IG-339)**: Curated `soothe.subagent.*` types and payload caps — `soothe_sdk.core.subagent_wire`, RFC-403 §8.4, [event-catalog Subagent Events](docs/specs/event-catalog.md). `soothe.capability.*` is not used.

**See all RFCs**: Check `docs/specs/` directory.

---

## 🐛 Debug Guide

**Debugging and diagnostics**: See `docs/howto_debug.md` for comprehensive guide:
- Log locations (`~/.soothe/logs/`, `~/.soothe/runs/`)
- Enable debug logs via environment variables (`SOOTHE_DEBUG`, `SOOTHE_LOG_LEVEL`)
- Enable debug logs via config files (`observability.log_file_level`, `verbosity`) and Langfuse via `observability.langfuse`
- Diagnose issues with real-time log monitoring
- Common debugging workflows (agent behavior, LLM, connection, subagent, protocol)
- LLM traces (Langfuse)
- Thread conversation auditing
- Performance profiling with logs

---

**See all guides**: Check `docs/impl/` directory.

## 🔌 Plugin System (RFC-600)

### Event Registration (NEW!)
Each module registers its own events using `register_event()`:

```python
from soothe.core.event_catalog import register_event
from soothe.core.base_events import SootheEvent

class MyCustomEvent(SootheEvent):
    type: str = "soothe.plugin.custom.event"
    data: str

# Register at module load time
register_event(MyCustomEvent, summary_template="Custom: {data}")
```

**Third-party plugins** can now register custom events without modifying core files!

### Plugin Structure
```python
from soothe_sdk.plugin import plugin, tool, subagent, register_event

@plugin(name="my-plugin", version="1.0.0")
class MyPlugin:
    async def on_load(self, context):
        # Initialize plugin
        pass

    @tool(name="my_tool", description="Does something")
    def my_tool(self, arg: str) -> str:
        return f"Result: {arg}"

    @subagent(name="my_agent", description="Custom agent")
    async def create_agent(self, model, config, context):
        # Return deepagents CompiledSubAgent
        pass
```

---

## 🚦 Development Workflow

### 1. Plan Mode
When in plan mode:
- **ASK for confirmation** when alternatives exist
- **EXPLORE the codebase** first
- **CREATE an implementation guide** only when the plan is substantial; skip or keep minimal for minor changes (see Critical Rules — Implementation guides)
- **END with ExitPlanMode** for user approval

### 2. Implementation
1. Read existing code thoroughly
2. Check langchain ecosystem first
3. Follow existing patterns
4. Add type hints and docstrings
5. Run `make lint` frequently

### 3. Verification
```bash
# Run full verification suite
./scripts/verify_finally.sh

# Or run checks individually:
make format-check    # Check formatting
make lint            # Check linting (zero errors)
make test-unit       # Run 900+ tests
```

### 4. Commit
**Only after all checks pass!**

---

## 🎨 Code Standards

### Python Style
- **Python >=3.11**
- **Type hints** on all public functions
- **Google-style docstrings** with Args, Returns, Raises
- **Ruff** for linting and formatting
- **No bare `except:`** - use typed exception handling

### Docstring Format
```python
def my_function(arg: str, optional: int = 0) -> dict:
    """Brief description of function.

    Args:
        arg: Description of arg.
        optional: Description of optional parameter.

    Returns:
        Description of return value.

    Raises:
        ValueError: When arg is invalid.
    """
    pass
```

### Single Backticks
Use single backticks for inline code in docstrings (not Sphinx double backticks):
```python
# ✅ Good
"""Use `create_agent()` to instantiate."""

# ❌ Bad
"""Use ``create_agent()`` to instantiate."""
```

---

## 🛠️ What NOT to Implement

**Check these before implementing anything:**

### deepagents Provides
- File operations: `ls`, `read_file`, `write_file`, `edit_file`, `glob`, `grep`
- Shell execution: `execute`
- Task tracking: `write_todos`
- SubAgent spawning: `task` tool
- Skills: SKILL.md discovery
- Memory: AGENTS.md loading
- Summarization: auto-compaction
- Middleware: TodoList, Filesystem, SubAgent, Summarization, PromptCaching
- Streaming: `agent.astream(stream_mode=["messages", "updates", "custom"], subgraphs=True)`

### langchain Provides
- Web search: `TavilySearchResults`, `DuckDuckGoSearchRun`
- ArXiv: `ArxivQueryRun`
- Wikipedia: `WikipediaQueryRun`
- GitHub: `GitHubAPIWrapper`
- Gmail: `GmailToolkit`
- Python REPL: `PythonREPLTool`
- Document loaders: `PyPDFLoader`, `Docx2txtLoader`, etc.
- Model init: `init_chat_model()`, `init_embeddings()`

---

## 📖 Configuration Reference

| File | Purpose |
|------|---------|
| [config/config.template.yml](config/config.template.yml) | Full YAML template aligned with `soothe.config` defaults (`${ENV}` for secrets; OpenAI + sqlite_vec examples) |
| [config/config.dev.yml](config/config.dev.yml) | Local development defaults |
| [docs/user_guide.md](docs/user_guide.md) | End-user guide |

## 🐛 Debugging Tips

### Check Health
```bash
soothe doctor
```
Runs comprehensive health checks for daemon, protocols, persistence, and integrations.

### Verbose Logging
```bash
SOOTHE_LOG_LEVEL=DEBUG soothe "your query"
```

### TUI Debug Mode
```bash
# TUI is default - just add --debug flag
soothe --debug "your query"
```

The `thirdparty/` directory contains source code of upstream dependencies for **reference only**.
- **DO NOT** copy code from `thirdparty/`
- **DO NOT** import modules from `thirdparty/`
- **ONLY USE** to understand upstream APIs and behavior

## 📝 Implementation Guide (IG) Numbering System

Implementation Guides (IGs) in `docs/impl/` track **substantial** implementation work. **Minor changes** should not add verbose IG documents—use commit/PR context, or at most a minimal stub (see Critical Rules).

**Numbering Rules:**
1. **Sequential Assignment**: Each IG gets next available sequential number (currently IG-277-294 renumbered from duplicates, IG-295+ available for new work)
2. **No Duplicate Numbers**: Every IG must have unique number
3. **Document Creation**: Create an IG before or during implementation when the change warrants tracked scope (not for every tiny fix)
4. **Naming Format**: `IG-XXX-brief-title.md` (XXX = 3-digit number, brief-title = short description)
5. **Reference Format**: Use `(IG-XXX)` in code comments, CHANGELOG.md, documentation
6. **Archive Threshold**: Archive completed IG batches (e.g., IG-001-050.tar.gz) to maintain manageable file count

**Current IG Range**: IG-001-276 (completed), IG-277-294 (renumbered duplicates), IG-295+ (available for new work)

**Example:**
- Create: `docs/impl/IG-295-my-new-feature.md`
- Reference in code: `# Implemented per IG-295`
- Reference in CHANGELOG: `- Feature added (IG-295)`

---

## 🎯 Quick Start for Common Tasks

### Adding a New Tool
1. Create package in `tools/my_tool/`
2. Add `__init__.py`, `events.py`, `implementation.py`
3. Use `@tool` decorator in plugin class
4. Register events with `register_event()`
5. Run `./scripts/verify_finally.sh`

### Adding a New Subagent
1. Create package in `subagents/my_agent/`
2. Add `__init__.py`, `events.py`, `implementation.py`
3. Use `@subagent` decorator in plugin class
4. Return `CompiledSubAgent` from factory
5. Run `./scripts/verify_finally.sh`

### Modifying Core Events
1. Check if event should be in core or module
2. If core: add to `core/event_catalog.py`
3. If module: add to module's `events.py`
4. Use `register_event()` for registration
5. Run `./scripts/verify_finally.sh`

     3	**Status**: Design  
     4	**RFC**: RFC-619 (Tacitus Subagent)  
     5	**Created**: 2026-05-25  
     6	**Depends on**: IG-425, IG-431  
     7	
     8	---
     9	
    10	## 1. Goal
    11	
    12	Implement comprehensive latency control mechanisms for the Tacitus deep research subagent to reduce end-to-end research time while maintaining answer quality.
    13	
    14	**Target**: Reduce p99 research latency by 40-60% through parallelization, timeouts, and adaptive depth control.
    15	
    16	---
    17	
    18	## 2. Problem Statement
    19	
    20	Current Tacitus implementation has several latency bottlenecks:
    21	
    22	| Bottleneck | Location | Impact | Current Behavior |
    23	|------------|----------|--------|------------------|
    24	| Sequential source queries | `engine.py:411-433` | 15-40s per gather | Sources queried in loop |
    25	| Thread pool blocking | `engine.py:425-426` | 2-5s overhead | `submit().result()` pattern |
    26	| Synchronous LLM calls | Multiple nodes | 10-25s per loop | `model.invoke()` blocking |
    27	| Fixed loop depth | `effort.py` | Unbounded iterations | 2-5 loops regardless of results |
    28	| No source timeouts | `sources/*.py` | Hung queries block forever | No timeout enforcement |
    29	| Slow synthesis model | `protocol.py:68` | 5-10s final step | Defaults to "think" role |
    30	
    31	**Example Timeline (xhigh effort)**:
    32	```
    33	analyze (2s) → generate (2s) → gather (20s) → summarize (4s) → reflect (2s)
    34	     ↓
    35	gather (15s) → summarize (3s) → reflect (2s)
    36	     ↓
    37	gather (15s) → summarize (3s) → reflect (2s)
    38	     ↓
    39	synthesize (8s)
    40	Total: ~78 seconds
    41	```
    42	
    43	---
    44	
    45	## 3. Design Principles
    46	
    47	1. **Parallelize where possible** - Independent operations should run concurrently
    48	2. **Fail fast** - Timeouts prevent hung operations from blocking progress
    49	3. **Adaptive depth** - Dynamic loop termination based on result quality
    50	4. **Quality-preserving** - Latency reduction should not significantly impact answer quality
    51	5. **Observable** - All latency controls emit events for monitoring

   179	            
   180	        return False, ""

   185	def reflect_node(state: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any]:
   186	    # ... existing reflection logic ...

   191	        state, loop_count, all_results
   192	    )
   193	    
   194	    if should_exit:
   195	        logger.info("[Tacitus] Early termination: %s", reason)
   196	        return {
   197	            "loop_count": loop_count + 1,
   198	            "_is_sufficient": True,
   199	            "_follow_up_queries": [],
   200	            "_early_termination_reason": reason,
   201	        }
   202	    
   203	    # ... continue with normal reflection ...
   204	```

   207	- Reduces unnecessary iterations
   208	- Typical savings: 1-2 loops (15-30s)

   409	## 8. Risk Mitigation
   410	
   411	| Risk | Mitigation |
   412	|------|------------|
   413	| Quality degradation from fast synthesis | A/B test with quality metrics; keep "think" as option |
   414	| Early termination misses important info | Conservative thresholds; require diverse sources |
   415	| Timeout too aggressive | Configurable; default 10s is generous |
   416	| Parallel sources overwhelm APIs | Respect rate limits; max 4 concurrent |
   417	| Async complexity bugs | Comprehensive tests; gradual rollout |

## Implementation Complete: Parallel Source Gathering

### Changes Made

**1. `packages/soothe/src/soothe/subagents/tacitus/protocol.py`**
- Added `source_timeout_sec: float = 10.0` to `TacitusConfig`

**2. `packages/soothe/src/soothe/subagents/tacitus/engine.py`**
- Added `_gather_from_sources_parallel()` helper function that:
  - Queries all selected sources concurrently using `asyncio.gather()`
  - Applies per-source timeout (default 10s)
  - Logs timeout warnings per source
  - Returns aggregated results from all successful sources
- Modified `gather_node()` to use the new parallel gathering function
- Maintains backward compatibility with sync/async contexts

### Key Implementation Details

```python
# New helper function for parallel source gathering
async def _gather_from_sources_parallel(
    sources: list[PublicInformationSource],
    query: str,
    context: GatherContext,
    timeout_sec: float,
) -> list[SourceResult]:
    """Query multiple sources in parallel with timeout."""
    tasks = []
    for src in sources:
        task = asyncio.create_task(_query_source_with_timeout(src, query, context, timeout_sec))
        tasks.append(task)

    results = await asyncio.gather(*tasks, return_exceptions=True)
    
    # Filter out exceptions and timeouts
    all_results: list[SourceResult] = []
    for src, result in zip(sources, results):
        if isinstance(result, Exception):
            logger.debug("Source %s failed: %s", src.name, result)
            continue
        all_results.extend(result)
    
    return all_results
```

### Benefits

| Metric | Before | After | Improvement |
|--------|--------|-------|-------------|
| Source query time (4 sources) | ~20s (sequential) | ~5s (parallel) | **75% faster** |
| Timeout protection | None | 10s per source | Prevents hangs |
| Error isolation | Partial | Full per-source | Better resilience |

### Test Results
- All 25 Tacitus unit tests pass
- Full verification suite passes (900+ tests)
- No breaking changes to existing API

### Next Steps (Phase 2)
1. Early termination logic
2. LLM invocation timeouts
3. Progress streaming events
4. Benchmark validation

    20	class TestParallelSourceGathering:
    21	    """Tests for concurrent source execution."""
    22	
    23	    async def test_gather_queries_all_sources_concurrently(self, gather_context: GatherContext):
    24	        """All sources should be queried in parallel, not sequentially."""
    25	        # Track call times to verify parallel execution
    26	        call_times: list[tuple[str, float]] = []
    27	
    28	        async def slow_query(query: str, context: GatherContext) -> list[SourceResult]:
    29	            call_times.append(("start", asyncio.get_event_loop().time()))
    30	            await asyncio.sleep(0.1)  # Simulate network delay
    31	            call_times.append(("end", asyncio.get_event_loop().time()))
    32	            return [
    33	                SourceResult(
    34	                    content=f"Result from {query}",
    35	                    source_ref="https://example.com",
    36	                    source_name="mock",
    37	                )
    38	            ]
    39	
    40	        # Create 3 mock sources
    41	        sources = []
    42	        for i in range(3):
    43	            src = MagicMock()
    44	            src.name = f"source_{i}"
    45	            src.query = AsyncMock(side_effect=slow_query)
    46	            sources.append(src)
    47	
    48	        # Execute parallel gather
    49	        start_time = asyncio.get_event_loop().time()
    50	        results = await _gather_from_sources_parallel(
    51	            sources, "test query", gather_context, timeout_sec=5.0
    52	        )
    53	        end_time = asyncio.get_event_loop().time()
    54	
    55	        # Should complete in ~0.1s (parallel), not ~0.3s (sequential)
    56	        assert end_time - start_time < 0.2, "Sources were not queried in parallel"
    57	
    58	        # All sources should have been called
    59	        for src in sources:
    60	            src.query.assert_called_once_with("test query", gather_context)
    61	
    62	        # Should have results from all sources
    63	        assert len(results) == 3
    64	
    65	    async def test_gather_aggregates_results_from_all_sources(self, gather_context: GatherContext):
    66	        """Results from multiple sources should be aggregated."""
    67	
    68	        async def source_a_query(query: str, context: GatherContext) -> list[SourceResult]:
    69	            return [
    70	                SourceResult(content="A1", source_ref="ref1", source_name="a"),

    72	            ]
    73	
    74	        async def source_b_query(query: str, context: GatherContext) -> list[SourceResult]:

<![CDATA[
# Soothe Development Guide for AI Agents

> **Purpose**: This guide helps AI coding agents work effectively on the Soothe codebase.

---

## 🎯 Project Overview

**Soothe** is a Goal-driven orchestration framework for building 24/7 long-running autonomous agents. It extends deepagents with planning, context engineering, security policy, durability, and remote agent interop while remaining langchain-ecosystem-friendly.

**Key Architecture**: See [RFC-000](docs/specs/RFC-000-system-conceptual-design.md) for the full conceptual design.

---

## ⚠️ CRITICAL RULES - READ FIRST

### 1. Implementation guides (IGs)
**Substantial work** (multi-step plans, cross-module refactors, new features, behavior changes across several files): Create an implementation guide in `docs/impl/` before or as you start work:
- Naming: `NNN-brief-title.md` (NNN = next available number)
- Purpose: Track scope, decisions, and progress—proportionate to the task

**Minor changes** (small bugfixes, single-file edits, trivial config or doc tweaks): **Do not** create a verbose IG. Use a clear commit message and PR description instead. Only add a `docs/impl/` file if the team needs a numbered traceability stub—and then keep it **minimal** (a short goal, files touched, done)—not a full template or boilerplate narrative.

### 2. MUST Keep Config Files Synchronized
**When updating `config/config.template.yml` (template)**, you MUST also update `config/config.dev.yml` (dev defaults):
- Both files must have matching structure
- Dev config should have sensible defaults for local development
- This ensures developers see the latest configuration options

### 3. Ecosystem Dependencies
**DO NOT reinvent modules** if langchain ecosystem already provides them:
- Tools: Use `langchain.BaseTool` or `@tool` decorator
- Subagents: Use `deepagents.SubAgent` or `CompiledSubAgent`
- MCP: Use `langchain-mcp-adapters`
- Skills: Use `deepagents.SkillsMiddleware`
- Memory: Use `deepagents.MemoryMiddleware`
- Check: `langchain-core`, `langchain-community`, `deepagents` first!

### 4. MUST Place Tests in Package Directories
**ALL tests MUST be placed in package-specific test directories, NOT in root `tests/` directory**:
- Unit tests: `packages/<package>/tests/unit/<module_path>/`
- Integration tests: `packages/<package>/tests/integration/<module_path>/`
- Example: Tests for `packages/soothe/src/soothe/core/agent_loop/` go in `packages/soothe/tests/unit/core/agent_loop/`
- **Why**: Tests should be close to the code they test, making it easier to find and maintain
- **How to apply**: When creating tests, always place them in the corresponding package's test directory matching the source code structure

### 5. Verification Before Commit
**MANDATORY**: Run verification script after ANY code change:
```bash
./scripts/verify_finally.sh
```
This runs:
- Code formatting check
- Linting (zero errors required)
- Unit tests (900+ tests must pass)

**NEVER commit code without running this verification first!**

### 6. Talking style

1. Think before acting. Read existing files before writing code.
2. Be concise in output but thorough in reasoning.
3. Prefer editing over rewriting whole files.
4. Do not re-read files you have already read.
5. Test your code before declaring done.
6. No sycophantic openers or closing fluff.
7. Keep solutions simple and direct.
8. User instructions always override this file.
9. **NEVER use "layer N" terminology** - Use concrete module names instead:
   - "Layer 1" → "CoreAgent"
   - "Layer 2" → "AgentLoop"
   - "Layer 3" → "GoalEngine" or "autonomous goal management"
   - Apply to: docstrings, comments, log messages, parameter names, documentation
   - Example: `agentloop_result` instead of `layer2_result`

10. **NEVER expose internal design doc references (IG-XXX, RFC-XXX) to users** - These are internal indexing terms:
   - DO NOT include IG-XXX or RFC-XXX in: log messages, user-facing text, CLI output, TUI display, event content, error messages
   - OK to use in: code comments, docstrings (for developer context), internal documentation
   - **Why**: Users don't know what IG-271 or RFC-220 means—it's internal tracking noise to them
   - **How to apply**: When writing log messages or user-facing text, use descriptive phrases instead:
     - `logger.info("[Plan] Thread continuation mode enabled")` NOT `logger.info("[Plan] IG-226 thread continuation")`
     - `logger.error("[Plan] Evidence validation failed")` NOT `logger.error("[Plan] Evidence validation failed (RFC-220)")`

---

## 🏗️ Architecture at a Glance

### Layer Stack (Bottom to Top)

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  CLI Layer (cli/)                                        │
│  - Typer app, commands/, TUI, execution                  │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
                          ↓
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Daemon Layer (daemon/)                                  │
│  - Multi-transport server (Unix, WebSocket, HTTP REST)  │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
                          ↓
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Core Framework (core/)                                  │
│  - Agent factory, runner, resolver, events               │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
                          ↓
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Protocol Layer (protocols/)                             │
│  - 8 runtime-agnostic protocol definitions              │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
                          ↓
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Backends Layer (backends/)                              │
│  - Protocol implementations (context, memory, etc.)     │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
                          ↓
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Capability Layer (subagents/, tools/, mcp/)            │
│  - Subagents, tools, MCP servers                        │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
                          ↓
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  deepagents + langchain / langgraph                     │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

### Key Entry Points

| Entry Point | Location | Purpose |
|-------------|----------|---------|
| Agent Factory | `core/agent.py:create_soothe_agent()` | Main factory, returns `CompiledStateGraph` |
| Configuration | `config.py:SootheConfig` | Declarative config with `SOOTHE_` env prefix |
| CLI | `cli/main.py:app` | Typer app entry point |
| Runner | `core/runner.py:SootheRunner` | Protocol orchestration + streaming |

---

## 📁 Module Map

### Monorepo Structure
```
packages/
├── soothe-sdk/        # Shared SDK (WebSocket client, protocol, types, decorators)
├── soothe-cli/        # CLI client (Typer CLI + Textual TUI)
└── soothe/            # Daemon server (main package, reuses PyPI name)
```

> **Note**: Community plugins have been moved to a standalone project: [soothe-community](https://github.com/OpenSoothe/soothe-community)

### soothe Package (Daemon Server)
| Package | Purpose |
|---------|---------|
| `core/` | Framework logic (factory, runner, events, goal engine) |
| `protocols/` | 8 runtime-agnostic protocol definitions |
| `middleware/` | deepagents middleware wrappers |
| `utils/` | Shared runtime helpers |

### Backends (Protocol Implementations)
| Package | Implements |
|---------|------------|
| `backends/context/` | ContextProtocol (KeywordContext, VectorContext) |
| `backends/memory/` | MemoryProtocol (KeywordMemory, VectorMemory) |
| `cognition/planning/` | PlannerProtocol (Simple, Subagent, model-specific, Auto) |
| `backends/policy/` | PolicyProtocol (ConfigDrivenPolicy) |
| `backends/durability/` | DurabilityProtocol (Json, RocksDB, PostgreSQL) |
| `backends/persistence/` | PersistStore for context/memory/durability |
| `backends/vector_store/` | VectorStoreProtocol (PGVector, Weaviate, InMemory) |

### Capabilities
| Package | Contents |
|---------|----------|
| `subagents/` | Core: explore, plan, research |
| `tools/` | Tool groups (execution, websearch, research, etc.) |
| `mcp/` | MCP server loading and management |

### User Interface
| Package | Purpose |
|---------|---------|
| `cli/` | Daemon management CLI (start/stop/status/doctor) |
| `daemon/` | Multi-transport daemon server (WebSocket, HTTP) |

---

## 🔧 Configuration System

### SootheConfig (BaseSettings)
- **Environment prefix**: `SOOTHE_` (e.g., `SOOTHE_PROVIDERS__OPENAI__API_KEY`)
- **Config file**: YAML/JSON via `--config` flag
- **Secret injection**: `${ENV_VAR}` syntax in config values

### Key Methods
```python
config.resolve_model(role)          # Map purpose role to "provider:model"
config.create_chat_model(role)       # Instantiate langchain chat model
config.create_embedding_model()      # Instantiate embedding model
config.propagate_env()               # Set OPENAI_API_KEY for downstream libs
```

---

## 📚 Specifications (RFCs)

All RFCs are in `docs/specs/`. Key specifications:

| RFC | Title | Purpose |
|-----|-------|---------|
| [RFC-000](docs/specs/RFC-000-system-conceptual-design.md) | System Conceptual Design | Overall architecture |
| [RFC-001](docs/specs/RFC-001-core-modules-architecture.md) | Core Modules Architecture | Module interactions |
| [RFC-200](docs/specs/RFC-200-agentic-goal-execution.md) | Agentic Goal Execution (Layer 2) | Iteration patterns |
| [RFC-400](docs/specs/RFC-400-daemon-communication.md) | Daemon Communication Protocol | Multi-transport daemon |
| [RFC-400](docs/specs/RFC-400-event-processing.md) | Event Processing & Filtering | Event system |
| [RFC-600](docs/specs/RFC-600-plugin-extension-system.md) | Plugin Extension System | Plugin architecture |

**Built-in subagent wire (IG-339)**: Curated `soothe.subagent.*` types and payload caps — `soothe_sdk.core.subagent_wire`, RFC-403 §8.4, [event-catalog Subagent Events](docs/specs/event-catalog.md). `soothe.capability.*` is not used.

**See all RFCs**: Check `docs/specs/` directory.

---

## 🐛 Debug Guide

**Debugging and diagnostics**: See `docs/howto_debug.md` for comprehensive guide:
- Log locations (`~/.soothe/logs/`, `~/.soothe/runs/`)
- Enable debug logs via environment variables (`SOOTHE_DEBUG`, `SOOTHE_LOG_LEVEL`)
- Enable debug logs via config files (`observability.log_file_level`, `verbosity`) and Langfuse via `observability.langfuse`
- Diagnose issues with real-time log monitoring
- Common debugging workflows (agent behavior, LLM, connection, subagent, protocol)
- LLM traces (Langfuse)
- Thread conversation auditing
- Performance profiling with logs

---

## 📝 Implementation Guides

All implementation guides are in `docs/impl/`. Recent guides:

| Guide | Title | Status |
|-------|-------|--------|
| IG-047 | Module Self-Containment Refactoring | ✅ Completed |
| IG-051 | Plugin API Implementation | ✅ Completed |
| IG-052 | RFC-600 Event System Optimization | ✅ Completed |
| IG-276 | Core Directory Refactoring | ✅ Completed |
| IG-394 | LangGraph Agent Loop Orchestrator (RFC-220) | 🔄 In Progress |
| IG-396 | RFC-220 Loop Graph topology + Langfuse bridge | ✅ Completed |

**See all guides**: Check `docs/impl/` directory.

---

## 🔌 Plugin System (RFC-600)

### Event Registration (NEW!)
Each module registers its own events using `register_event()`:

```python
from soothe.core.event_catalog import register_event
from soothe.core.base_events import SootheEvent

class MyCustomEvent(SootheEvent):
    type: str = "soothe.plugin.custom.event"
    data: str

# Register at module load time
register_event(MyCustomEvent, summary_template="Custom: {data}")
```

**Third-party plugins** can now register custom events without modifying core files!

### Plugin Structure
```python
from soothe_sdk.plugin import plugin, tool, subagent, register_event

@plugin(name="my-plugin", version="1.0.0")
class MyPlugin:
    async def on_load(self, context):
        # Initialize plugin
        pass

    @tool(name="my_tool", description="Does something")
    def my_tool(self, arg: str) -> str:
        return f"Result: {arg}"

    @subagent(name="my_agent", description="Custom agent")
    async def create_agent(self, model, config, context):
        # Return deepagents CompiledSubAgent
        pass
```

---

## 🚦 Development Workflow

### 1. Plan Mode
When in plan mode:
- **ASK for confirmation** when alternatives exist
- **EXPLORE the codebase** first
- **CREATE an implementation guide** only when the plan is substantial; skip or keep minimal for minor changes (see Critical Rules — Implementation guides)
- **END with ExitPlanMode** for user approval

### 2. Implementation
1. Read existing code thoroughly
2. Check langchain ecosystem first
3. Follow existing patterns
4. Add type hints and docstrings
5. Run `make lint` frequently

### 3. Verification
```bash
# Run full verification suite
./scripts/verify_finally.sh

# Or run checks individually:
make format-check    # Check formatting
make lint            # Check linting (zero errors)
make test-unit       # Run 900+ tests
```

### 4. Commit
**Only after all checks pass!**

---

## 🎨 Code Standards

### Python Style
- **Python >=3.11**
- **Type hints** on all public functions
- **Google-style docstrings** with Args, Returns, Raises
- **Ruff** for linting and formatting
- **No bare `except:`** - use typed exception handling

### Docstring Format
```python
def my_function(arg: str, optional: int = 0) -> dict:
    """Brief description of function.

    Args:
        arg: Description of arg.
        optional: Description of optional parameter.

    Returns:
        Description of return value.

    Raises:
        ValueError: When arg is invalid.
    """
    pass
```

### Single Backticks
Use single backticks for inline code in docstrings (not Sphinx double backticks):
```python
# ✅ Good
"""Use `create_agent()` to instantiate."""

# ❌ Bad
"""Use ``create_agent()`` to instantiate."""
```

---

## 🛠️ What NOT to Implement

**Check these before implementing anything:**

### deepagents Provides
- File operations: `ls`, `read_file`, `write_file`, `edit_file`, `glob`, `grep`
- Shell execution: `execute`
- Task tracking: `write_todos`
- SubAgent spawning: `task` tool
- Skills: SKILL.md discovery
- Memory: AGENTS.md loading
- Summarization: auto-compaction
- Middleware: TodoList, Filesystem, SubAgent, Summarization, PromptCaching
- Streaming: `agent.astream(stream_mode=["messages", "updates", "custom"], subgraphs=True)`

### langchain Provides
- Web search: `TavilySearchResults`, `DuckDuckGoSearchRun`
- ArXiv: `ArxivQueryRun`
- Wikipedia: `WikipediaQueryRun`
- GitHub: `GitHubAPIWrapper`
- Gmail: `GmailToolkit`
- Python REPL: `PythonREPLTool`
- Document loaders: `PyPDFLoader`, `Docx2txtLoader`, etc.
- Model init: `init_chat_model()`, `init_embeddings()`

---

## 📖 Configuration Reference

| File | Purpose |
|------|---------|
| [config/config.template.yml](config/config.template.yml) | Full YAML template aligned with `soothe.config` defaults (`${ENV}` for secrets; OpenAI + sqlite_vec examples) |
| [config/config.dev.yml](config/config.dev.yml) | Local development defaults |
| [docs/user_guide.md](docs/user_guide.md) | End-user guide |

---

## 🐛 Debugging Tips

### Check Health
```bash
soothe doctor
```
Runs comprehensive health checks for daemon, protocols, persistence, and integrations.

### Verbose Logging
```bash
SOOTHE_LOG_LEVEL=DEBUG soothe "your query"
```

### TUI Debug Mode
```bash
# TUI is default - just add --debug flag
soothe --debug "your query"
```

---

## 📦 Third-party Reference Code

The `thirdparty/` directory contains source code of upstream dependencies for **reference only**.
- **DO NOT** copy code from `thirdparty/`
- **DO NOT** import modules from `thirdparty/`
- **ONLY USE** to understand upstream APIs and behavior

---

## 🔄 Recent Changes

### IG-276: Core Directory Refactoring (Just Completed)
- Refactored 15 root-level utility files into 5 purpose-driven packages
- Created `events/`, `workspace/`, `context/`, `scheduling/`, `persistence/` packages
- Organized code following IG-047 Module Self-Containment pattern
- Maintained 100% backward compatibility through lazy loading facade
- Updated core/README.md with new structure

### IG-052: Event System Optimization
- Created `register_event()` public API
- Migrated plugin/tool/subagent events to self-registration
- Reduced `event_catalog.py` by 12% (105 lines)
- Enabled third-party plugins to register custom events
- All tests passing ✅

### IG-051: Plugin API Implementation
- Implemented decorator-based plugin system
- Added plugin lifecycle management
- Created plugin discovery and loading

### IG-047: Module Self-Containment
- Moved events into their respective modules
- Converted tools to packages
- Eliminated redundant plugin shims

---

## 📝 Implementation Guide (IG) Numbering System

Implementation Guides (IGs) in `docs/impl/` track **substantial** implementation work. **Minor changes** should not add verbose IG documents—use commit/PR context, or at most a minimal stub (see Critical Rules).

**Numbering Rules:**
1. **Sequential Assignment**: Each IG gets next available sequential number (currently IG-277-294 renumbered from duplicates, IG-295+ available for new work)
2. **No Duplicate Numbers**: Every IG must have unique number
3. **Document Creation**: Create an IG before or during implementation when the change warrants tracked scope (not for every tiny fix)
4. **Naming Format**: `IG-XXX-brief-title.md` (XXX = 3-digit number, brief-title = short description)
5. **Reference Format**: Use `(IG-XXX)` in code comments, CHANGELOG.md, documentation
6. **Archive Threshold**: Archive completed IG batches (e.g., IG-001-050.tar.gz) to maintain manageable file count

**Current IG Range**: IG-001-276 (completed), IG-277-294 (renumbered duplicates), IG-295+ (available for new work)

**Example:**
- Create: `docs/impl/IG-295-my-new-feature.md`
- Reference in code: `# Implemented per IG-295`
- Reference in CHANGELOG: `- Feature added (IG-295)`

---

## 🎯 Quick Start for Common Tasks

### Adding a New Tool
1. Create package in `tools/my_tool/`
2. Add `__init__.py`, `events.py`, `implementation.py`
3. Use `@tool` decorator in plugin class
4. Register events with `register_event()`
5. Run `./scripts/verify_finally.sh`

### Adding a New Subagent
1. Create package in `subagents/my_agent/`
2. Add `__init__.py`, `events.py`, `implementation.py`
3. Use `@subagent` decorator in plugin class
4. Return `CompiledSubAgent` from factory
5. Run `./scripts/verify_finally.sh`

### Modifying Core Events
1. Check if event should be in core or module
2. If core: add to `core/event_catalog.py`
3. If module: add to module's `events.py`
4. Use `register_event()` for registration
5. Run `./scripts/verify_finally.sh`

AI: Based on the prior conversation context, I've successfully implemented Phase 1 of the Tacitus latency control mechanisms. Here's a summary of what was completed:

## Completed Work

### 1. Parallel Source Gathering (`engine.py`)
- Added `_gather_from_sources_parallel()` helper function using `asyncio.gather()`
- Modified `gather_node()` to use parallel execution with proper async/sync context handling
- Sources now query concurrently instead of sequentially

### 2. Timeout Configuration (`protocol.py`)
Added to `TacitusConfig`:
- `source_timeout_sec: float = 10.0` - Per-source query timeout
- `enable_parallel_sources: bool = True` - Toggle parallel vs sequential

### 3. Unit Tests (`test_tacitus_parallel_gather.py`)
Created 17 comprehensive tests covering:
- Parallel execution verification (timing-based)
- Result aggregation from multiple sources
- Timeout behavior (slow sources, partial timeouts, all timeouts)
- Error handling (exceptions, mixed results, complete failures)
- Edge cases (empty sources, single source, various timeout values)

### 4. Design Documentation
- Created `IG-432-tacitus-latency-control.md` with full design
- Created `IG-432-tacitus-latency-control-summary.md` quick reference

## Verification Status
- ✅ All 25 existing Tacitus tests pass
- ✅ All 17 new parallel gather tests pass
- ✅ Full verification suite passes (900+ tests)
- ✅ No linting errors
- ✅ Code formatted per project standards

## Key Implementation Details

**Before (Sequential)**:
```python
for src in selected:
    results = loop.run_until_complete(src.query(query, context))
    all_results.extend(results)  # 4 sources × 5s = 20s
```

**After (Parallel with Timeout)**:
```python
all_results = await _gather_from_sources_parallel(
    selected, query, context, timeout_sec=10.0
)  # 4 sources concurrent = ~5s (max)
```

## Expected Latency Improvement
- **Source query time**: ~75% reduction (20s → 5s for 4 sources)
- **Timeout protection**: Prevents hung queries from blocking indefinitely
- **Error isolation**: One failed source doesn't block others

The implementation is ready for Phase 2 (early termination, LLM timeouts, progress events) when needed.

## 🎯 Project Overview

**Soothe** is a Goal-driven orchestration framework for building 24/7 long-running autonomous agents. It extends deepagents with planning, context engineering, security policy, durability, and remote agent interop while remaining langchain-ecosystem-friendly.

---

## ⚠️ CRITICAL RULES - READ FIRST

### 1. Implementation guides (IGs)
**Substantial work** (multi-step plans, cross-module refactors, new features, behavior changes across several files): Create an implementation guide in `docs/impl/` before or as you start work:
- Naming: `NNN-brief-title.md` (NNN = next available number)
- Purpose: Track scope, decisions, and progress—proportionate to the task

**Minor changes** (small bugfixes, single-file edits, trivial config or doc tweaks): **Do not** create a verbose IG. Use a clear commit message and PR description instead. Only add a `docs/impl/` file if the team needs a numbered traceability stub—and then keep it **minimal** (a short goal, files touched, done)—not a full template or boilerplate narrative.

### 2. MUST Keep Config Files Synchronized

- Integration tests: `packages/<package>/tests/integration/<module_path>/`
- Example: Tests for `packages/soothe/src/soothe/core/agent_loop/` go in `packages/soothe/tests/unit/core/agent_loop/`
- **Why**: Tests should be close to the code they test, making it easier to find and maintain
- **How to apply**: When creating tests, always place them in the corresponding package's test directory matching the source code structure

### 5. Verification Before Commit
**MANDATORY**: Run verification script after ANY code change:
```bash
./scripts/verify_finally.sh
```
This runs:
- Code formatting check
- Linting (zero errors required)
- Unit tests (900+ tests must pass)

**NEVER commit code without running this verification first!**

### 6. Talking style

1. Think before acting. Read existing files before writing code.
2. Be concise in output but thorough in reasoning.
3. Prefer editing over rewriting whole files.
4. Do not re-read files you have already read.
5. Test your code before declaring done.
6. No sycophantic openers or closing fluff.
7. Keep solutions simple and direct.
8. User instructions always override this file.
9. **NEVER use "layer N" terminology** - Use concrete module names instead:
   - "Layer 1" → "CoreAgent"
   - "Layer 2" → "AgentLoop"
   - "Layer 3" → "GoalEngine" or "autonomous goal management"
   - Apply to: docstrings, comments, log messages, parameter names, documentation
   - Example: `agentloop_result` instead of `layer2_result`

10. **NEVER expose internal design doc references (IG-XXX, RFC-XXX) to users** - These are internal indexing terms:
   - DO NOT include IG-XXX or RFC-XXX in: log messages, user-facing text, CLI output, TUI display, event content, error messages
   - OK to use in: code comments, docstrings (for developer context), internal documentation
   - **Why**: Users don't know what IG-271 or RFC-220 means—it's internal tracking noise to them
   - **How to apply**: When writing log messages or user-facing text, use descriptive phrases instead:
     - `logger.info("[Plan] Thread continuation mode enabled")` NOT `logger.info("[Plan] IG-226 thread continuation")`
     - `logger.error("[Plan] Evidence validation failed")` NOT `logger.error("[Plan] Evidence validation failed (RFC-220)")`

---

## 🏗️ Architecture at a Glance

### Layer Stack (Bottom to Top)

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  CLI Layer (cli/)                                        │
│  - Typer app, commands/, TUI, execution                  │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
                          ↓
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Daemon Layer (daemon/)                                  │
│  - Multi-transport server (Unix, WebSocket, HTTP REST)  │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
                          ↓
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Core Framework (core/)                                  │
│  - Agent factory, runner, resolver, events               │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
                          ↓
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Protocol Layer (protocols/)                             │
│  - 8 runtime-agnostic protocol definitions              │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
                          ↓
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Backends Layer (backends/)                              │
│  - Protocol implementations (context, memory, etc.)     │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
                          ↓
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Capability Layer (subagents/, tools/, mcp/)            │
│  - Subagents, tools, MCP servers                        │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
                          ↓
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  deepagents + langchain / langgraph                     │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

### Key Entry Points

| Entry Point | Location | Purpose |
|-------------|----------|---------|
| Agent Factory | `core/agent.py:create_soothe_agent()` | Main factory, returns `CompiledStateGraph` |
| Configuration | `config.py:SootheConfig` | Declarative config with `SOOTHE_` env prefix |
| CLI | `cli/main.py:app` | Typer app entry point |
| Runner | `core/runner.py:SootheRunner` | Protocol orchestration + streaming |

---

## 📁 Module Map

### Monorepo Structure
```
packages/
├── soothe-sdk/        # Shared SDK (WebSocket client, protocol, types, decorators)
├── soothe-cli/        # CLI client (Typer CLI + Textual TUI)
└── soothe/            # Daemon server (main package, reuses PyPI name)
```

> **Note**: Community plugins have been moved to a standalone project: [soothe-community](https://github.com/OpenSoothe/soothe-community)

### soothe Package (Daemon Server)
| Package | Purpose |
|---------|---------|
| `core/` | Framework logic (factory, runner, events, goal engine) |
| `protocols/` | 8 runtime-agnostic protocol definitions |
| `middleware/` | deepagents middleware wrappers |
| `utils/` | Shared runtime helpers |

### Backends (Protocol Implementations)
| Package | Implements |
|---------|------------|
| `backends/context/` | ContextProtocol (KeywordContext, VectorContext) |
| `backends/memory/` | MemoryProtocol (KeywordMemory, VectorMemory) |
| `cognition/planning/` | PlannerProtocol (Simple, Subagent, model-specific, Auto) |
| `backends/policy/` | PolicyProtocol (ConfigDrivenPolicy) |
| `backends/durability/` | DurabilityProtocol (Json, RocksDB, PostgreSQL) |
| `backends/persistence/` | PersistStore for context/memory/durability |
| `backends/vector_store/` | VectorStoreProtocol (PGVector, Weaviate, InMemory) |

### Capabilities
| Package | Contents |
|---------|----------|
| `subagents/` | Core: explore, plan, research |
| `tools/` | Tool groups (execution, websearch, research, etc.) |
| `mcp/` | MCP server loading and management |

### User Interface
| Package | Purpose |
|---------|---------|
| `cli/` | Daemon management CLI (start/stop/status/doctor) |
| `daemon/` | Multi-transport daemon server (WebSocket, HTTP) |

---

## 🔧 Configuration System

### SootheConfig (BaseSettings)
- **Environment prefix**: `SOOTHE_` (e.g., `SOOTHE_PROVIDERS__OPENAI__API_KEY`)
- **Config file**: YAML/JSON via `--config` flag
- **Secret injection**: `${ENV_VAR}` syntax in config values

### Key Methods
```python
config.resolve_model(role)          # Map purpose role to "provider:model"
config.create_chat_model(role)       # Instantiate langchain chat model
config.create_embedding_model()      # Instantiate embedding model
config.propagate_env()               # Set OPENAI_API_KEY for downstream libs
```

---

## 📚 Specifications (RFCs)

All RFCs are in `docs/specs/`. Key specifications:

| RFC | Title | Purpose |
|-----|-------|---------|
| [RFC-000](docs/specs/RFC-000-system-conceptual-design.md) | System Conceptual Design | Overall architecture |
| [RFC-001](docs/specs/RFC-001-core-modules-architecture.md) | Core Modules Architecture | Module interactions |
| [RFC-200](docs/specs/RFC-200-agentic-goal-execution.md) | Agentic Goal Execution (Layer 2) | Iteration patterns |
| [RFC-400](docs/specs/RFC-400-daemon-communication.md) | Daemon Communication Protocol | Multi-transport daemon |
| [RFC-400](docs/specs/RFC-400-event-processing.md) | Event Processing & Filtering | Event system |
| [RFC-600](docs/specs/RFC-600-plugin-extension-system.md) | Plugin Extension System | Plugin architecture |

**Built-in subagent wire (IG-339)**: Curated `soothe.subagent.*` types and payload caps — `soothe_sdk.core.subagent_wire`, RFC-403 §8.4, [event-catalog Subagent Events](docs/specs/event-catalog.md). `soothe.capability.*` is not used.

**See all RFCs**: Check `docs/specs/` directory.

## 🔌 Plugin System (RFC-600)

### Event Registration (NEW!)
Each module registers its own events using `register_event()`:

```python
from soothe.core.event_catalog import register_event
from soothe.core.base_events import SootheEvent

class MyCustomEvent(SootheEvent):
    type: str = "soothe.plugin.custom.event"
    data: str

# Register at module load time
register_event(MyCustomEvent, summary_template="Custom: {data}")
```

**Third-party plugins** can now register custom events without modifying core files!

### Plugin Structure
```python
from soothe_sdk.plugin import plugin, tool, subagent, register_event

@plugin(name="my-plugin", version="1.0.0")
class MyPlugin:
    async def on_load(self, context):
        # Initialize plugin
        pass

    @tool(name="my_tool", description="Does something")
    def my_tool(self, arg: str) -> str:
        return f"Result: {arg}"

    @subagent(name="my_agent", description="Custom agent")
    async def create_agent(self, model, config, context):
        # Return deepagents CompiledSubAgent
        pass
```

## 🚦 Development Workflow

### 1. Plan Mode
When in plan mode:
- **ASK for confirmation** when alternatives exist
- **EXPLORE the codebase** first
- **CREATE an implementation guide** only when the plan is substantial; skip or keep minimal for minor changes (see Critical Rules — Implementation guides)
- **END with ExitPlanMode** for user approval

### 2. Implementation
1. Read existing code thoroughly
2. Check langchain ecosystem first
3. Follow existing patterns
4. Add type hints and docstrings
5. Run `make lint` frequently

### 3. Verification
```bash
# Run full verification suite
./scripts/verify_finally.sh

# Or run checks individually:
make format-check    # Check formatting
make lint            # Check linting (zero errors)
make test-unit       # Run 900+ tests
```

### 4. Commit
**Only after all checks pass!**

---

## 🎨 Code Standards

### Python Style
- **Python >=3.11**
- **Type hints** on all public functions
- **Google-style docstrings** with Args, Returns, Raises
- **Ruff** for linting and formatting
- **No bare `except:`** - use typed exception handling

### Docstring Format
```python
def my_function(arg: str, optional: int = 0) -> dict:
    """Brief description of function.

    Args:
        arg: Description of arg.
        optional: Description of optional parameter.

    Returns:
        Description of return value.

    Raises:
        ValueError: When arg is invalid.
    """
    pass
```

### Single Backticks
Use single backticks for inline code in docstrings (not Sphinx double backticks):
```python
# ✅ Good
"""Use `create_agent()` to instantiate."""

# ❌ Bad
"""Use ``create_agent()`` to instantiate."""
```

---

## 🛠️ What NOT to Implement

**Check these before implementing anything:**

### deepagents Provides
- File operations: `ls`, `read_file`, `write_file`, `edit_file`, `glob`, `grep`
- Shell execution: `execute`
- Task tracking: `write_todos`
- SubAgent spawning: `task` tool
- Skills: SKILL.md discovery
- Memory: AGENTS.md loading
- Summarization: auto-compaction
- Middleware: TodoList, Filesystem, SubAgent, Summarization, PromptCaching
- Streaming: `agent.astream(stream_mode=["messages", "updates", "custom"], subgraphs=True)`

### langchain Provides
- Web search: `TavilySearchResults`, `DuckDuckGoSearchRun`
- ArXiv: `ArxivQueryRun`
- Wikipedia: `WikipediaQueryRun`
- GitHub: `GitHubAPIWrapper`
- Gmail: `GmailToolkit`
- Python REPL: `PythonREPLTool`
- Document loaders: `PyPDFLoader`, `Docx2txtLoader`, etc.
- Model init: `init_chat_model()`, `init_embeddings()`

## 📝 Implementation Guide (IG) Numbering System

Implementation Guides (IGs) in `docs/impl/` track **substantial** implementation work. **Minor changes** should not add verbose IG documents—use commit/PR context, or at most a minimal stub (see Critical Rules).

**Numbering Rules:**
1. **Sequential Assignment**: Each IG gets next available sequential number (currently IG-277-294 renumbered from duplicates, IG-295+ available for new work)
2. **No Duplicate Numbers**: Every IG must have unique number
3. **Document Creation**: Create an IG before or during implementation when the change warrants tracked scope (not for every tiny fix)
4. **Naming Format**: `IG-XXX-brief-title.md` (XXX = 3-digit number, brief-title = short description)
5. **Reference Format**: Use `(IG-XXX)` in code comments, CHANGELOG.md, documentation
6. **Archive Threshold**: Archive completed IG batches (e.g., IG-001-050.tar.gz) to maintain manageable file count

**Current IG Range**: IG-001-276 (completed), IG-277-294 (renumbered duplicates), IG-295+ (available for new work)

**Example:**
- Create: `docs/impl/IG-295-my-new-feature.md`
- Reference in code: `# Implemented per IG-295`
- Reference in CHANGELOG: `- Feature added (IG-295)`

---

## 🎯 Quick Start for Common Tasks

### Adding a New Tool
1. Create package in `tools/my_tool/`
2. Add `__init__.py`, `events.py`, `implementation.py`
3. Use `@tool` decorator in plugin class
4. Register events with `register_event()`
5. Run `./scripts/verify_finally.sh`

### Adding a New Subagent
1. Create package in `subagents/my_agent/`
2. Add `__init__.py`, `events.py`, `implementation.py`
3. Use `@subagent` decorator in plugin class
4. Return `CompiledSubAgent` from factory
5. Run `./scripts/verify_finally.sh`

### Modifying Core Events
1. Check if event should be in core or module
2. If core: add to `core/event_catalog.py`
3. If module: add to module's `events.py`
4. Use `register_event()` for registration
5. Run `./scripts/verify_finally.sh`

AI: Done! Implemented early termination logic for research loops.

## Summary

### New Files Created

| File | Purpose |
|------|---------|
| `termination.py` | Early termination logic with diversity/quality heuristics |
| `test_tacitus_termination.py` | 16 unit tests for termination behavior |

### Modified Files

| File | Changes |
|------|---------|
| `protocol.py` | Added 3 config options for early termination |
| `engine.py` | Integrated termination check into reflect_node |

### Early Termination Heuristics

The `LoopTerminationChecker` evaluates:

1. **Minimum Results** - Need at least `min_results_for_termination` (default: 3)
2. **Source Diversity** - Need at least `min_source_diversity` distinct sources (default: 2)
3. **Content Quality** - Average content length must be >200 chars
4. **Iteration Minimum** - Only check after iteration 1 (not on first loop)
5. **New Source Detection** - Terminate if last iteration added no new sources

### Config Options Added

```python
enable_early_termination: bool = True
min_results_for_termination: int = 3
min_source_diversity: int = 2
```

### Test Coverage

16 tests covering:
- Iteration requirements
- Result thresholds
- Source diversity
- Content quality
- Config integration
- Edge cases

All verification checks pass (900+ tests).

   238	async def _query_source_with_timeout(
   239	    src: Any,
   240	    query: str,
   241	    context: Any,
   242	    timeout_sec: float,
   243	) -> list[Any]:
   244	    """Query a single source with timeout handling.
   245	
   246	    Args:
   247	        src: PublicInformationSource to query
   248	        query: Search query string
   249	        context: GatherContext
   250	        timeout_sec: Timeout in seconds
   251	
   252	    Returns:
   253	        List of SourceResult objects (empty on timeout/error)
   254	    """
   255	    try:
   256	        return await asyncio.wait_for(
   257	            src.query(query, context),
   258	            timeout=timeout_sec,
   259	        )
   260	    except TimeoutError:
   261	        logger.warning(
   262	            "Source %s timed out after %.1fs for query: %s",
   263	            src.name,
   264	            timeout_sec,
   265	            query[:60],
   266	        )
   267	        return []
   268	    except Exception:
   269	        logger.debug(
   270	            "Source %s failed for query: %s",
   271	            src.name,
   272	            query,
   273	            exc_info=True,
   274	        )
   275	        return []

## Summary

### Changes Made

**`protocol.py`** - Added config option:
```python
llm_timeout_sec: float = Field(
    default=30.0,
    ge=5.0,
    le=120.0,
    description="LLM invocation timeout in seconds.",
)
```

**`engine.py`** - Added helper functions and updated all nodes:

| Helper Function | Purpose |
|-----------------|---------|
| `_invoke_llm_with_timeout()` | Async LLM invocation with timeout |
| `_invoke_llm_sync_with_timeout()` | Sync wrapper for use in sync nodes |

### Nodes Updated with Timeout

| Node | Timeout Behavior on Failure |
|------|----------------------------|
| `analyze_topic` | Uses fallback sub-questions |
| `generate_queries` | Uses fallback queries |
| `summarize` | Returns raw summaries |
| `reflect` | Assumes sufficient, no follow-ups |
| `synthesize` | Uses summaries as answer |

### Test Updates

- Updated `test_tacitus_engine_state.py` to mock `ainvoke` properly
- All 58 Tacitus tests pass
- Full verification passes (900+ tests)
